# The Algiers Darija Book — الدارجة الدزيرية
### A vocabulary book for AI: Algiers Darija in Arabic letters and in Latin letters with French, the way people in Algiers (Dzayer) really talk.

Version 6 — November 2026 · 37 chapters · full grammar and spelling rules · Standard Arabic → Darija table · regions · 3 dictionaries (Darija → English: 1888 entries · English → Darija: 1803 · French → Darija: 619)

This book holds **vocabulary and language rules only**. How to sell, chat scripts, product names and prices live in the **guide de vente**.

**How the AI uses this book (fast and cheap):** the AI does not read the whole book for every message. It always has the short **pocket card** (Darija_Pocket_Card.md), and a **word finder** looks up only the words found in each message from this book. See the README of the AI kit.

---

## How to read this book (for the AI)

- **Dialect**: Algiers (الدزيرية, "Dziri"). This is Algerian Darija. It is **not** Moroccan (Darija ta3 lmghrib) and **not** Tunisian (Tounsi). Chapter 3 lists the words that give away Moroccan or Tunisian speech, so you never use them.
- **Two ways of writing**: every word is given in Arabic letters **and** in Latin letters ("Arabizi"), the way Algerians type on phones.
- **Meaning** is given in simple English. Sometimes French is added, because Algerians mix French into Darija all the time.
- **Honest note**: this book was written from knowledge of how Algerians speak, not from watching every series or reading private groups. Any line marked ⚠️ should be checked by a native speaker from Algiers.

### Latin letters code (Arabizi) — Algerian style

| Number / letters | Arabic letter | Sound | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | ع | deep "a" from the throat | 3lach — علاش (why) |
| 7 | ح | strong breathy "h" | 7aja — حاجة (thing) |
| 9 | ق | "q" from the back of the throat | 9ahwa — قهوة (coffee) |
| 5 or kh | خ | like Spanish "j" | khobz / 5obz — خبز (bread) |
| gh | غ | like French "r" | ghodwa — غدوة (tomorrow) |
| ch | ش | "sh" | chouf — شوف (look) |
| j / dj | ج | "j" (Algiers says j, like French "je") | jib — جيب (bring) |
| ou / w | و | "oo" / "w" | rouh — روح (go) |
| 2 | ء | small stop | rarely used |
| 8 | ه | rarely used; Algerians just write "h" | — |

**Important**: in Algeria, **9 = ق** (not ص like in the Middle East). "Wa9tach" = وقتاش (when).

**Algiers sound**: in the city of Algiers, ق is said as a real "q": **9al** (he said), **9a3ed** (sitting). People from the countryside and many other regions say "g": "gal", "ga3ed". For Algiers style, use **9**. The one big exception everybody uses: **ga3** (all) — قاع.

# Chapter 1 — The 100 words you need first

### 1.1 Question words

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| واش | wach / wech | what? / also starts a yes-no question |
| شكون | chkoun | who? |
| وين | win | where? |
| منين | mnin | from where? |
| وقتاش | wa9tach | when? |
| علاش | 3lach | why? |
| كيفاش | kifach | how? |
| شحال | ch7al / chhal | how much? / how many? |
| أناهو / أناهي / أناهوما | anahou / anahi / anahouma | which one (m) / which one (f) / which ones |
| بواش | bwach | with what? |
| لواش | lwach | for what? / what for? |

Examples:
- واش راك؟ — wach rak? — How are you? (to a man)
- وين راك؟ — win rak? — Where are you?
- شحال هذا؟ — ch7al hada? — How much is this?
- علاش ما جيتش؟ — 3lach ma jitch? — Why didn't you come?
- أناهو اللي تحب؟ — anahou elli t7ab? — Which one do you want?

### 1.2 "I am / you are" — the Algerian "ra-" words

This is one of the most Algerian things. "Rani" means "I am (right now)".

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| راني | rani | I am |
| راك | rak | you are (man) |
| راكي | raki | you are (woman) |
| راه | rah | he is / it is |
| راهي | rahi | she is / it is (f) |
| رانا | rana | we are |
| راكم | rakom | you are (group, or polite) |
| راهم | rahom | they are |

- راني نخدم — rani nkhdem — I'm working.
- راني جاي — rani jay — I'm coming.
- راهي غالية — rahi ghalya — It's expensive.
- راكم مليح؟ — rakom mli7? — Are you (all) fine?

### 1.3 "Of / belongs to" — تاع (ta3)

Algerians say **ta3**. (Moroccans say "dyal". Never use "dyal".)

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| تاعي | ta3i | mine |
| تاعك | ta3ek | yours |
| تاعو | ta3ou | his |
| تاعها | ta3ha | hers |
| تاعنا | ta3na | ours |
| تاعكم | ta3kom | yours (plural) |
| تاعهم | ta3hom | theirs |

- الكرهبة تاعي — el karhba ta3i — my car.
- الكوموند تاعك وصلت — la commande ta3ek wesslet — your order arrived.
- هذا تاع من؟ — hada ta3 men? — Whose is this?

### 1.4 Yes, no, maybe

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| إيه | ih | yes |
| واه | wah | yes (very common, relaxed) |
| لالا / لا | lala / la | no |
| نورمال | normal | sure / of course / no problem |
| ساهل | sahel | easy / no problem |
| صحا | sa7a | OK / fine / thanks (depends on context) |
| دكور / دكورد | d'accord / dakor | OK |
| واخا ❌ | (wakha) | Moroccan — do NOT use |
| بلاك | balak | maybe |
| يمكن | ymken | maybe / it's possible |
| إن شاء الله | inchallah / nchallah | God willing (hope / maybe yes) |
| ماشي مشكل | machi mochkil | no problem |
| خلاص | khlas | done / that's it / enough |
| برك | bark | only / that's all / stop |

### 1.5 Time words

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| درك | dorka | now |
| دوك | dok | right now / in a second ("dok nji" = I'll come right now) |
| ضرك ضرك | dorka dorka | right now, immediately |
| اليوم | el youm | today |
| غدوة | ghodwa | tomorrow |
| بعد غدوة | ba3d ghodwa | the day after tomorrow |
| البارح | lbare7 | yesterday |
| ولّ البارح | wel lbare7 | the day before yesterday |
| الصباح / الصباح بكري | sba7 / sba7 bekri | morning / early morning |
| القايلة | l9ayla | early afternoon, the hot hours |
| العشية | la3chiya | afternoon / evening |
| الليل | lil | night |
| ديما | dima | always |
| ساعات | sa3at | sometimes |
| عمري ما | 3omri ma | never ("3omri ma chafto" = I never saw him) |
| مازال | mazal | still / not yet |
| ماشي درك | machi dorka | not now |
| فيسع / بالخف | fissa3 / belkhef | quickly |
| شوية شوية | chwiya chwiya | slowly / little by little |
| من بكري | men bekri | since long ago |
| السيمانة | simana | week (French "semaine") |
| الشهر | chhar | month |
| العام | el 3am | year |

**Days**: el 7ad (Sunday), letnin (Monday), tlata (Tuesday), larb3a (Wednesday), lakhmis (Thursday), ljem3a (Friday), sebt (Saturday).
Arabic: الحد، الاثنين، الثلاثاء، الأربعاء، الخميس، الجمعة، السبت.
In Algeria the weekend is **Friday + Saturday**. Sunday is a work day.

### 1.6 Quantity words

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| بزاف | bezzaf | a lot / too much / very |
| شوية | chwiya | a little |
| قاع | ga3 | all / everything / at all ("ma fhemt ga3" = I didn't understand at all) |
| كامل | kamel | all / whole ("kamel el wilayat" = all the wilayas) |
| والو | walou | nothing |
| حتى حاجة | 7ata 7aja | nothing at all |
| غير | ghir | only / just |
| زيد | zid | more / add / go on |
| نقص | n9es | less / reduce |
| بزاف عليا | bezzaf 3liya | too much for me |
| كيف كيف | kif kif | the same |
| تاني | tani | also / too |

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |

| بصح | bessah | but |
| ولا | wella | or |
| باه / باش | bah / bach | so that / in order to ("bach" also = "with") |
| كي | ki | when / like ("ki tousel" = when you arrive) |
| كيما | kima | like / as |
| حتى | 7ata | until / even |
| إذا / كان | ida / kan | if |
| على جال | 3la jal | because of / for the sake of |
| خاطر / على خاطر | khater / 3la khater | because |

| يعني | ya3ni | I mean / so |
| المهم | lmouhim | anyway / the main thing |
| هنا | hna | here |
| لهيه | lhih | over there (Algiers) |
| تم / تما | temma | there |
| مع | m3a | with |
| بلا | bla | without |
| فوق / تحت | fou9 / ta7t | on / under |
| قدام / ورا | 9oddam / wra | in front / behind |
| حدا | 7da | next to |

### 1.8 Must-know verbs (in "I" form, then "you")

In Algerian Darija, "I" verbs start with **n-** (nakul = I eat). "We" verbs start with **n-** and end with **-ou** (naklou = we eat). This is a Maghreb feature.

| Arabic | Latin (I / you m / you f) | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| نحب | n7ab / t7ab / t7abi | want / love / like |
| نروح | nrou7 / trou7 / trou7i | go |
| نجي | nji / tji / tji | come |
| أرواح | arwa7 / arwa7i | come here! (Algiers) |
| نشوف | nchouf / tchouf / tchoufi | see / look |
| نقول | n9oul / t9oul / t9ouli | say |
| نهدر | nahder / tahder / tahdri | talk |
| نفهم | nefhem / tefhem / tefhmi | understand |
| نعرف | na3ref / ta3ref / ta3rfi | know |
| نقدر | n9der / t9der / t9adri | can |
| ندير | ndir / tdir / tdiri | do / make / put |
| نجيب | njib / tjib / tjibi | bring |
| ندي | nddi / tddi / tddi | take (away) |
| نعطي | na3ti / ta3ti / ta3ti | give |
| نشري | nechri / techri / techri | buy |
| نبيع | nbi3 / tbi3 / tbi3i | sell |
| نخلص | nkhalles / tkhalles / tkhallsi | pay |
| ناكل | nakul / takul / takli | eat |
| نشرب | nechrob / techrob / techorbi | drink |
| نرقد | nar9ed / tar9ed / tar9di | sleep |
| نخدم | nkhdem / tkhdem / tkhedmi | work |
| نقرا | n9ra / t9ra / t9ray | study / read |
| نلعب | nel3ab / tel3ab / tel3bi | play |
| نستنا | nestenna / testenna / testennay | wait |
| نعيط | n3ayet / t3ayet / t3ayti | call (on the phone) / shout |
| نسقسي | nsa9si / tsa9si / tsa9si | ask (a question) |
| نبدل | nbeddel / tbeddel / tbeddli | change / exchange |
| نرجع | nraja3 / traja3 / traj3i | return / give back (also "nwalli" = come back) |
| نولي | nwalli / twalli / twalli | come back / become |
| نخرج | nokhroj / tokhroj / tokhorji | go out |
| ندخل | nodkhol / todkhol / todkhli | go in |
| نسكن | nesken / tesken / teskni | live (somewhere) |
| نصيب | nsib / tsib / tsibi | find |
| نتلف | ntlef / ttlef / ttelfi | get lost / lose ("tlefli" = I lost it) |
| نزقل | nzeggel / tzeggel | miss (a bus, a chance) ("zgalt el bus") |
| نسمع | nesma3 / tesma3 / tesm3i | hear / listen |
| نحكم | n7akem / t7akem | catch / hold |
| نخمم | nkhammem / tkhammem / tkhammi | think |
| يعجبني | ya3jebni | I like it ("ya3jebni bezzaf" = I like it a lot) |

Past tense, simple:
- I did = **dert** (درت); you did = **dert / derti**; he did = **dar**; she did = **dart**; we did = **derna**; they did = **dar(ou)**.
- I went = **rou7t**; I came = **jit**; I ate = **klit**; I bought = **chrit**; I paid = **khallast**; I saw = **chuft**; I said = **9olt**.

Future: Algerians do **not** use Moroccan "ghadi". They say:
- **rani rayeh** + verb: راني رايح نشري — rani rayeh nechri — I'm going to buy.
- Or just the present + a time word: غدوة نجي — ghodwa nji — I'll come tomorrow.

### 1.9 How to say "no / not"

- Verbs: **ma … ch**. ما نحبش — ma n7abch — I don't want. ما فهمتش — ma fhemtch — I didn't understand.
- Not (with words/adjectives): **machi**. ماشي غالية — machi ghalya — it's not expensive.
- There isn't: **makach** (ماكانش / ماكاش). ماكانش الطاي — makach la taille — the size isn't available.
- I don't know: **ma3labalich** (ماعلاباليش) — very Algerian. Also: ma na3refch.
- Never: **3omri ma**.
- Nobody: **7ata wa7ed**. Nothing: **walou**.

### 1.10 Numbers

| # | Arabic | Latin |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | واحد | wa7ed |
| 2 | زوج | zouj |
| 3 | ثلاثة | tlata |
| 4 | أربعة | rab3a |
| 5 | خمسة | khamsa |
| 6 | ستة | setta |
| 7 | سبعة | sab3a |
| 8 | ثمنية | tmenya |
| 9 | تسعة | tes3a |
| 10 | عشرة | 3achra |
| 11 | حداش | 7dach |
| 12 | ثناش | tnach |
| 13 | ثلطاش | tlettach |
| 14 | ربعطاش | rba3tach |
| 15 | خمسطاش | khamstach |
| 16 | سطاش | settach |
| 17 | سبعطاش | sba3tach |
| 18 | ثمنطاش | tmentach |
| 19 | تسعطاش | tsa3tach |
| 20 | عشرين | 3achrin |
| 30 | ثلاثين | tlatin |
| 40 | أربعين | rb3in |
| 50 | خمسين | khamsin |
| 100 | مية | mya |
| 200 | ميتين | mitin |
| 300 | ثلاث مية | tlatmya |
| 1 000 | ألف | alf |
| 2 000 | ألفين | alfin |
| 1 000 000 | مليون | mlyoun |

"Two of something" uses **zouj**: zouj sbabet (two pairs of shoes), zouj tfal (two kids).
Algerians very often say numbers in **French** too: "trois mille", "quarante-deux" (shoe size), "deux pièces".

# Chapter 2 — Greetings, politeness and respect

### 2.1 Hello and how are you

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning / when |
|---|---|---|
| السلام عليكم | salam 3likoum | hello (most common, always safe) |
| وعليكم السلام | wa 3likoum salam | reply to salam |
| صباح الخير | sba7 el khir | good morning |
| مسا الخير | msa el khir | good evening |
| صلام / سلام | salam / slm | hi (text) |
| كيراك؟ | kirak? | how are you? (m) — very Algiers |
| كيراكي؟ | kiraki? | how are you? (f) |
| كيراكم؟ | kirakom? | how are you all? / polite |
| واش راك؟ / واش راكي؟ | wach rak? / wach raki? | how are you? |
| لاباس؟ | labas? | all good? |
| لاباس الحمد لله | labas, l7amdoullah | fine, thank God |
| الحمد لله | l7amdoullah / hamdoulah | thank God (answer to "how are you") |
| واش راك داير؟ | wach rak dayer? | what are you up to / how's it going? |
| واش الأخبار؟ | wach lakhbar? | what's new? |
| كاش جديد؟ | kach jdid? | anything new? |
| ça va? | sava? | ça va? (very common) |
| مرحبا بيك / بيكي / بيكم | mar7ba bik / biki / bikom | welcome |
| توحشناك | twa7chnak | we missed you |
| وين راك غابر؟ | win rak ghaber? | where have you been? (long time no see) — Algerian |

### 2.2 Thank you

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| صحا / صحيت | sa7a / sa7it | thanks (sa7it = "well done, thanks") |
| يعطيك الصحة | ya3tik sa7a | thank you (lit. God give you health) — the main Algerian thanks |
| يعطيكي الصحة | ya3tiki sa7a | thank you (to a woman) |
| يعطيكم الصحة | ya3tikom sa7a | thank you (to a group / polite) |
| يعيشك | y3aychek | thank you (lit. may God let you live) — very Algerian |
| بارك الله فيك | barak allahou fik | thank you, God bless you |
| ميرسي | merci / mrc / mer6 | thanks (text) |
| الله يخليك | allah ykhalik | thank you / please (lit. God keep you) |
| ربي يخليك | rabbi ykhalik | same, warm |
| الله يحفظك | allah y7afdek | God protect you (warm thanks) |
| **Answer**: | | |
| الله يسلمك | allah ysalmek | answer to "ya3tik sa7a" |
| بلا مزية | bla mziya | you're welcome (no favor) |
| ماشي مشكل | machi mochkil | no problem |
| حتى حاجة | 7ata 7aja | it's nothing |

### 2.3 Please, sorry, excuse me

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| الله يخليك / ربي يخليك | allah ykhalik / rabbi ykhalik | please |
| يرحم باباك | yer7am babak | please! (lit. God bless your father) — very Algerian, friendly-urgent |
| يرحم والديك | yer7am waldik | please (lit. God bless your parents) |
| من فضلك | men fadlek | please (more formal) |
| سيلفوبلي | s'il vous plaît / svp / stp | please (text, very common) |
| سمحلي / سمحيلي / سمحولي | sme7li / sam7ili / sam7ouli | sorry / excuse me (m / f / group) |
| معليش | ma3lich | never mind / it's OK / sorry |
| ديزولي | désolé / dsl | sorry (text) |
| الله غالب | allah ghaleb | nothing can be done (sad acceptance) |
| ما عليهش | ma 3lihch | no harm / it's OK |

### 2.4 What to call people (very important)

| Arabic | Latin | For whom |
|---|---|---|
| خويا | khouya | "my brother" — man of similar age (safe) |
| ختي / أختي | khti / oukhti | "my sister" — woman (safe) |
| خو / كو | kho | bro (young men, casual) |
| صاحبي | sa7bi | my friend / buddy (young men) |
| فريرو | frero | bro (young, from French "frère") |
| مدام | madame | adult woman (polite) |
| الحاج / الحاجة | el 7ajj / el 7ajja | older man / older woman (respect) |
| عمي | 3ammi | older man (uncle, respect) |
| خالتي | khalti | older woman (auntie, respect) |
| يا الشيخ | ya chikh | older man (friendly respect) |
| يا وليدي / يا بنتي | ya wlidi / ya benti | my son / my daughter (old person talking to young) |

**Never say to a stranger**: habibti, 3omri, zina, ya ghzala, ya 9albi. These are flirting or too close. Avoid "mademoiselle".

# Chapter 3 — Algerian, NOT Moroccan, NOT Tunisian

These three dialects share many words, but some words instantly show "this is not Algerian". If you use them, an Algerian feels it is fake.

### 3.1 Big giveaway words

| Meaning | ✅ Algeria (Algiers) | ❌ Morocco | ❌ Tunisia |
|---|---|---|---|
| I want | n7ab — نحب | bghit — بغيت | n7eb — نحب (same root, different sound) |
| now | dorka — درك | daba — دابا | taw — تو |
| OK | sa7a / d'accord / normal | wakha — واخا | behi — باهي |
| good | mli7 — مليح | mzyan — مزيان | behi — باهي |
| a lot / very | bezzaf — بزاف | bzzaf (shared) | barcha — برشا |
| of / belongs to | ta3 — تاع | dyal — ديال | mta3 — متاع |
| what? | wach — واش | ach / chno — آش / شنو | chnowa / chniya — شنوة |
| where? | win — وين | fin — فين | win (shared) |
| when? | wa9tach — وقتاش | imta — إمتى | wa9tech |
| how much? | ch7al — شحال | bch7al — بشحال | 9addech — قداش |
| I don't know | ma3labalich — ماعلاباليش | ma3reftch — ماعرفتش | ma na3refch |
| money | drahem — دراهم | flous — فلوس | flous — فلوس |
| please | allah ykhalik / svp | 3afak — عافاك | 3aychek |
| here | hna — هنا | hna | houni — هوني |
| also | tani — تاني | 7ta — حتى | zeda — زادة |
| future "will" | rani rayeh n… | ghadi n… — غادي | bech n… — باش |
| to look/see | chouf | chouf (shared) | chouf |
| a little | chwiya | chwiya (shared) | chwaya |
| car | tonobil / loto / karhba | tomobil / tonobil | karhba |
| tomorrow | ghodwa | ghedda — غدا | ghodwa |
| nothing | walou | walou (shared) | 7atta chay |
| thank you | ya3tik sa7a / sa7it | lah ykhlik / choukran | ya3tik sa77a / 3aychek |
| "how are you?" | kirak? / wach rak? | labas? / kidayr? | chnowa a7welek? / la bes? |
| let's go | yallah / yaw | yallah | yezzi / ayya |
| I'm tired | rani 3yan | ana 3yan | ana t3abt |

### 3.2 Words from the Middle East to avoid
- ktir (Levant "a lot") → say **bezzaf**
- aywa, keda, ezzayak (Egypt) → say **ih / wah, hakka, kirak**
- abshir, ya 3omri, wayed (Gulf) → say **sa7a, khouya, bezzaf**
- "yalla bina" is fine, but "yalla habibi" is Middle-East flirting style → avoid.
- Do **not** write in Standard Arabic (Fus7a) to a normal person: «عزيزي العميل»، «يرجى تزويدنا» sound like a robot from a ministry. Many Algerians find it cold.

### 3.3 East and West of Algeria (only to understand, not to write)
You write Algiers style, but people from all regions will write to you. Understand them:
- West (Oran, Tlemcen): "wach rak dayer", "bezzaf", future with "ghadi" sometimes, "drouk" (now), "nta3" … "ta3", "gal" (with g), "rayeh" … "rouh", "ki rak". "Ya kho"/"ya ouldi".
- East (Constantine, Annaba, Sétif): "wach rak" also, "chbik?" (what's wrong?), "taw" sometimes in the far east near Tunisia, "barcha" rarely, "9a3ed" → "ga3ed", "nta" → "enti" for both genders sometimes, "zeda" (also).
- Kabylie: many speak Kabyle (Tamazight) and French. "Azul" = hello, "Tanemmirt" = thank you. If someone writes "Azul", answer "Azul! Tanemmirt" and continue in French or simple Darija.

---

# Chapter 4 — Small grammar, made easy

### 4.1 Talking to a man, a woman, or a group

| To a man | To a woman | To a group / polite |
|---|---|---|
| nta — نتا (you) | nti — نتي | ntouma — نتوما |
| rak — راك | raki — راكي | rakom — راكم |
| t7ab — تحب | t7abi — تحبي | t7abou — تحبو |
| chouf — شوف (look!) | choufi — شوفي | choufou — شوفو |
| ya3tik sa7a | ya3tiki sa7a | ya3tikom sa7a |
| bik | biki | bikom |
| 3lik | 3liki | 3likom |

**Rule for the AI**: if you don't know if the person is a man or a woman, use the **group form** (rakom, t7abou, ya3tikom sa7a) or French "vous". It's polite and safe.

### 4.2 Pronouns

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| أنا | ana | I |
| نتا / نتي | nta / nti | you (m / f) |
| هو / هي | howa / hiya | he / she |
| حنا | 7na | we |
| نتوما | ntouma | you (plural) |
| هوما | houma | they |

### 4.3 Endings for "my / your / his"

- -i (my): dari (my house), khouya (my brother — special), yemma (my mother — special)
- -ek (your): darek, tilifounek
- -ou (his): darou
- -ha (her): darha
- -na (our): darna
- -kom (your plural): darkom
- -hom (their): darhom

Same endings after words: **3lik** (on you), **m3ak** (with you), **lik / lek** (for you), **fik** (in you).
- "nab3athlek" = I'll send you. "3ayetli" = call me. "ab3atli" = send me. "9olli" = tell me.

### 4.4 This / that

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| هذا / هادا | hada | this (m) |
| هذي / هادي | hadi | this (f) |
| هذو / هادو | hadou | these |
| هذاك | hadak | that (m) |
| هذيك | hadik | that (f) |
| هذوك | hadouk | those |

### 4.5 Asking questions softly

Algerians often start with **wach** for yes/no questions:
- واش كاين التوصيل لوهران؟ — wach kayen ttawsil l wahran? — Is there delivery to Oran?
- واش مازال؟ — wach mazal? — Is it still available?
- كاش …؟ — kach …? — Is there any…? ("kach taille 40?")

### 4.6 There is / there isn't

- كاين / كاينة — kayen / kayna — there is / it's available (m / f)
- ماكانش / ماكاش — makach — there isn't
- مازال كاين — mazal kayen — still available
- خلاص، كملت — khlas, kemlet — finished, sold out ("kemlet" = it's finished)
- راهي مقطوعة — rahi ma9tou3a — it's out of stock (lit. cut)

---

# Chapter 5 — Full grammar rules of Algiers Darija

All the rules, step by step, with examples. Chapter 4 is the short version; this is the full one.

### 5.1 "The": el / l and the doubled letter

- "The" is **el** or **l**: el bab (the door), l'karhba (the car), lma (the water).
- Before the letters t, d, r, z, s, ch, n, l (and ث ذ ص ض ط ظ), the "l" disappears and the next letter is **doubled**: **ddar** (the house), **ssou9** (the market), **nnas** (the people), **ttri9** (the road), **chchems** (the sun), **rrajel** (the man), **zzit** (the oil).
- In Arabic letters you still write ال: الدار، السوق، الناس.
- French words keep "le / la / les" OR take "el / l": "la commande" or "l commande", "le colis" or "el colis". Both are normal.

### 5.2 Masculine and feminine nouns

- Most feminine words end in **-a** (written ة): tabla (table), karhba (car), chkara (bag), mra (woman), djaja (hen).
- Some feminine words have no -a: dar (house), chems (sun), yed (hand), 3in (eye), rjel (leg).
- French words keep their French gender: la voiture → "tonobil" is feminine; le colis is masculine.
- Adjectives follow the noun: "tonobil **jdida**" (a new car), "bab **kbir**" (a big door).

### 5.3 Plurals

**A. Add -in (people, jobs, adjectives):** khddam → khddamin (workers), mrid → mridin (sick people), fer7an → fer7anin.

**B. Add -at (feminine words, French words):** tabla → tablat, karhba → karhbat ⚠️, la commande → les commandes / commandat, tonobil → tonobilat, sa3a → sa3at.

**C. Change inside the word (broken plurals) — must learn:**

| One | Many | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| dar | dyar | house |
| bab | biban | door |
| bit | byout | room |
| kteb | ktouba | book |
| tebsi | tbassi | plate |
| korsi | krasa / kraci | chair |
| kas | kissan | glass |
| sbe3 | swaba3 | finger |
| wled | wlad | boy / son |
| bent | bnat | girl / daughter |
| rajel | rjal | man |
| mra | nsa | woman |
| khou | khawa | brother |
| okht | khawatat | sister |
| sa7bi | s7abi | friend |
| tfol | drari | child |
| jar | jiran | neighbour |
| youm | ayyam / iyyam | day |
| chhar | chhour | month |
| 3am | snin / 3wam | year |
| sa3a | swaye3 / sa3at | hour |
| kelb | klab | dog |
| 9att | 9tout | cat |
| jbel | jbal | mountain |
| kbir | kbar | big |
| sghir | sghar | small |
| twil | twal | tall |
| jdid | jdad | new |
| 9dim | 9dam | old |

**D. Two of something:** zouj + plural: "zouj drari" (2 kids), "zouj tonobilat". Time words have a special "two" ending -ayn: **youmayn** (2 days), **sa3tayn** (2 hours), **chahrayn** (2 months), **3amayn** (2 years), **alfayn / alfin** (2 000).

**E. Numbers 3 to 10 + plural:** tlata ayyam (3 days), khamsa drari (5 kids), 3achra dinar.
**Numbers 11 and up + singular:** 3achrin youm (20 days), mya dinar (100 DA), alf dinar.

### 5.4 My, your, his: endings

| | after a consonant | after a vowel | example (dar = house) | example (khou = brother) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| my | -i | -ya | dari | khouya |
| your (m/f) | -ek | -k | darek | khouk |
| his | -ou | -h / -ah | darou | khouh |
| her | -ha | -ha | darha | khouha |
| our | -na | -na | darna | khouna |
| your (pl) | -kom | -kom | darkom | khoukom |
| their | -hom | -hom | darhom | khouhom |

- Family words always use endings: yemma (my mother), baba / bouya (my father), khouya, khti, rajli (my husband), marti (my wife), wlidi (my son).
- Objects can use endings or **ta3**: "tilifouni" = "ttilifoun ta3i" (my phone). With French words, use **ta3**: "la voiture ta3i", "le compte ta3ek".
- Feminine -a becomes -t before endings: karhba → karhbti (my car), chkara → chkartek (your bag), tabla → tablathom.

### 5.5 Me, you, him (object endings on verbs)

| Ending | Meaning | Example (chaf = he saw) |
|---|---|---|
| -ni | me | chafni (he saw me) |
| -ek / -k | you | chafek (he saw you) |
| -ou / -h | him / it | chafou (he saw him) |
| -ha | her / it (f) | chafha |
| -na | us | chafna |
| -kom | you (pl) | chafkom |
| -hom | them | chafhom |

**To me / for me (l-):** 9olli (tell me), 9ollek (I tell you), 9ollou (tell him), 9olha (tell her), 9ollna (tell us), 9ollhom (tell them). Ab3atli (send me), ab3atlek (I'll send you), jibli (bring me), dirli (do for me), 7elli (open for me).

**Negative with endings:** ma chafnich (he didn't see me), ma 9oltlekch (I didn't tell you).

### 5.6 Verbs — present tense (now / habit)

Model: **kteb** (write).

| Person | Form | Example |
|---|---|---|
| I | n-kteb | nekteb |
| you (m) | t-kteb | tekteb |
| you (f) | t-ktb-i | tektbi |
| he | y-kteb | yekteb |
| she | t-kteb | tekteb |
| we | n-ktb-ou | nektbou |
| you (pl) | t-ktb-ou | tektbou |
| they | y-ktb-ou | yektbou |

Rule: **n-** = I (and we + -ou), **t-** = you / she, **y-** = he / they. The "we" form with **n…ou** is typical of the Maghreb.

Verbs ending in a vowel (mcha = go/walk, kla = eat, ja = come, chra = buy):

| | walk | eat | come | buy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | nemchi | nakul | nji | nechri |
| you (m) | temchi | takul | tji | techri |
| you (f) | temchi | takli | tji | techri |
| he | yemchi | yakul | yji | yechri |
| she | temchi | takul | tji | techri |
| we | nemchiw | naklou | njiw | nechriw |
| you (pl) | temchiw | taklou | tjiw | techriw |
| they | yemchiw | yaklou | yjiw | yechriw |

### 5.7 Verbs — past tense

Model **kteb**:

| Person | Form |
|---|---|
| I | ktebt |
| you (m) | ktebt |
| you (f) | ktebti |
| he | kteb |
| she | ketbet |
| we | ktebna |
| you (pl) | ktebtou |
| they | ketbou |

Note: in Algiers **"I wrote" and "you (m) wrote" sound the same (ktebt)**. Context tells which.

Vowel verbs: mchit / mchit / mchiti / mcha / mchat / mchina / mchitou / mchaw (walk). klit / klit / kliti / kla / klat / klina / klitou / klaw (eat). jit / jit / jiti / ja / jat / jina / jitou / jaw (come). chrit / chrit / chriti / chra / chrat / chrina / chritou / chraw (buy).

Hollow verbs (middle vowel): **rah** (he went): rou7t / rou7t / rou7ti / ra7 / ra7et / rou7na / rou7tou / ra7ou. **chaf** (he saw): chuft / chuft / chufti / chaf / chafet / chufna / chuftou / chafou. **kan** (he was): kont / kont / konti / kan / kanet / konna / kontou / kanou.

### 5.8 Commands

| | Man | Woman | Group |
|---|---|---|---|
| write | kteb | kotbi | kotbou |
| go | rou7 | rou7i | rou7ou |
| come | arwa7 | arwa7i | arwa7ou |
| eat | koul | kouli | koulou |
| give me | a3tini | a3tini | a3touni |
| look | chouf | choufi | choufou |

**Don't …:** ma + t-verb + ch: ma tekteb**ch** (don't write, m), ma tkotbi**ch** (f), ma tkotbou**ch** (pl). Ma trou7ch (don't go), ma tkhafch (don't be afraid), ma t9ale9ch (don't worry).

### 5.9 Right now, going to, used to

- **Right now**: rani / rak / rah… + present verb: "rani nakul" (I'm eating), "rahi tahder" (she's talking).
- **Participles (state now)**: rayeh / rayha (going), jay / jaya (coming), galess (sitting), wa9ef (standing), ra9ed (asleep), fahem (understanding: "rak fahem?"), 3aref (knowing: "rani 3aref"), chayef (seeing), msafer (travelling), m9alle9 (upset), mestenni (waiting) ⚠️.
- **Future**: "rani rayeh + verb" (I'm going to…): "rani rayeh nechri" (I'm going to buy). Or present + time word: "ghodwa nji". **Do not** use Moroccan "ghadi" or Tunisian "bech" for the future in Algiers style.
- **Used to / was doing**: kan + present: "kont nkhdem f Oran" (I used to work in Oran), "kanet t9ra" (she was studying).
- **Will be**: ykoun / tkoun / nkoun: "nkoun f dar" (I'll be at home).

### 5.10 Saying "no"

| Use | Rule | Example |
|---|---|---|
| verbs | ma … ch | ma fhemtch (I didn't understand), ma n7abch (I don't want) |
| words, adjectives, participles | machi | machi ghali (not expensive), machi fahem (not understanding) |
| there isn't | makach / makanch | makach la taille |
| I don't have | ma 3andich | ma 3andich drahem |
| not yet | mazal ma … | mazal ma ja (he hasn't come yet) |
| nothing / nobody | ma … walou / ma … 7ata wa7ed | ma chuft walou, ma ja 7ata wa7ed |
| at all | ma … ga3 | ma klit ga3 |
| never | 3omri ma … | 3omri ma chuftou |
| none left | mab9ach | mab9ach walou (there's nothing left) |

### 5.11 Questions

- Yes/no: start with **wach**: "wach rak jay?" (are you coming?). Or just raise the voice / add "?": "rak jay?"
- With question words: wach (what), chkoun (who), win (where), mnin (from where), wa9tach (when), 3lach (why), kifach (how), ch7al (how much/many), anahou / anahi (which).
- "Or": wella: "9ahwa wella atay?"
- Tags at the end: "… s7i7?" (right?), "… hakka?" (like this?), "… non?"
- "Is there any…?": kach / kayen …?: "kach jdid?", "kayen taille 40?"

### 5.12 "To have"

3and + ending: **3andi** (I have), 3andek, 3andou, 3andha, 3andna, 3andkom, 3andhom.
- Past: kan 3andi (I had). Future: ykoun 3andi (I'll have).
- Not: ma 3andich, ma 3andekch, ma 3andouch…
- "I need": **yekhessni** (يخصني): "yekhessni drahem" (I need money), "yekhessek tkhalles" ⚠️ (you need to pay). Moroccan "khassni" is not Algiers style.

### 5.13 Want, can, must

- **n7ab + verb** (I want to…): "n7ab nrou7" — no word for "to" between the verbs.
- **n9der + verb** (I can…): "n9der nji ghodwa", "ma n9derch".
- **lazem + verb** (must): "lazem nrou7 dorka".
- **ykhessni / yekhessni** (I need to).
- **balak** (maybe), **ymken** (possibly).

### 5.14 Little words (prepositions)

| Latin | Arabic | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| f / fi | في | in / at | f dar (at home) |
| l / li | لـ | to / for | rou7t l Oran |
| men | من | from / than | jit men l'khedma; kbir men |
| m3a | مع | with | m3a sa7bi |
| b | بـ | with / by / for (price) | b ttri9; b alf dinar |
| 3la | على | on / about / for | 3la tabla; 3la khatrek |
| 3and | عند | at someone's | 3and khalti |
| 7ata | حتى | until / even | 7ata ghodwa |
| bla | بلا | without | bla sokkor |
| 9bel / ba3d | قبل / بعد | before / after | 9bel lftour |
| 9oddam / wra / mor | قدام / ورا / مور | in front / behind | |
| fou9 / ta7t | فوق / تحت | on / under | |
| bin | بين | between | |
| 7da / jenb | حدا / جنب | next to | |
| kima / kif | كيما / كيف | like / as | kima hada |

### 5.15 Joining words

w (and) · wella (or) · bessah (but) · khater / 3la khater (because) · 3la jal (because of) · bach / bah (so that) · ida / kan / lukan (if — "lukan" for "if only") · ki (when) · kima (as) · 7ata (until) · 9bel ma (before) · mor ma / ba3d ma (after) · ya … ya (either … or) · donc / alors (so) · même si (even if).

### 5.16 Describing and comparing

- Adjective after the noun and agrees: "drari sghar", "bent sghira".
- With "the": both get el: "**l**karhba **l**7amra" (the red car).
- **Comparing**: short "more" form + **men**: "kbar men" (bigger than), "sghar men" (smaller than), "twal men" (taller than), "ghla men" (dearer than), "rkhes men" (cheaper than), "khir men" (better than).
- **The most**: "khir wa7ed" (the best one), "kbir wa7ed" (the biggest), or French "le plus …".
- **Very**: bezzaf after the adjective: "mli7 bezzaf".

### 5.17 This, that

- hada (this, m), hadi (this, f), hadou (these), hadak (that, m), hadik (that, f), hadouk (those).
- Before a noun: **had** + el-noun: "had ttonobil" (this car), "had ssimana" (this week). Or "ttonobil hadi".
- "This is it": hada houwa / hadi hiya.

### 5.18 The "ra-" word

**ra- + ending** points to a situation now: rani, rak, raki, rah, rahi, rana, rakom, rahom.
- With adjectives: "rahi ghalya" (it's expensive), "rani 3yan" (I'm tired).
- With verbs: "rani nkhdem" (I'm working).
- With places: "rani f dar" (I'm at home).
- "Rah ja" = he has (just) come.

### 5.19 Word order

Normal: who + verb + what: "Karim chra tonobil." Also verb first: "chra Karim tonobil." For focus, put the topic first: "Ttonobil, chraha Karim" (the car, Karim bought it).

### 5.20 French words inside verbs

French verb + Darija ending **-i**: ncommandi, tcommandi, ycommandi, commandit (I ordered), commanda (he ordered), commandaw (they ordered). Same for: nconfirmi, nannuli, nreservi, ncharji, npartagi, ntelechargi, nbloqui, nparki, nchangi, ndépassi. Negative: "ma commanditch" (I didn't order).

### 5.21 Small sound rules

- Short vowels often disappear: "ktebt" not "katabtu"; "tkhalles" not "tukhallisu".
- An "e" is added to help pronounce: nekteb, tektbi.
- Long words get squeezed: "ma 3labalich" (I don't know) from "ma 3la bali ch".
- Algiers says **9** (ق) clearly: 9al, 9a3ed, 9ahwa. Exception: **ga3** (all) and some words with **g** (gless, gezzar, garantita).

---

# Chapter 6 — Spelling rules: writing Darija in Arabic letters and in Latin letters

### 6.1 Arabic letters

1. **Write how people speak, not Standard Arabic**: واش (not ماذا), راني (not أنا), ماكانش (not لا يوجد), درك (not الآن).
2. **"Not" is attached**: ما نحبش، ما فهمتش، ماكانش (ma … ch = ما … ش).
3. **"The" (ال) stays written** even when it's not said: الدار (ddar), السوق (ssou9).
4. **Small words stick to the next word**: لـ (to), بـ (with), و (and): للدار، بالخف، وخويا. "في" can be written في or ف.
5. **Feminine -a** = ة at the end: طابلة، كرهبة، شكارة. Before endings it becomes ت: كرهبتي.
6. **Special letters**: ڨ for the "g" sound (ڨاع، ڨلس), ڤ for "v" (ڤوكال، ڤيلو) — many people just write ق and ف.
7. **French words in Arabic letters**, written as heard: الكوموند (la commande), الليفرور (le livreur), الكولي (le colis), البوسطة (la poste), الفاكتورة (la facture).
8. No short-vowel marks (harakat). Use ؟ for questions and ، for commas.
9. Prices in digits: 3900 دج.

### 6.2 Latin letters (Arabizi)

1. **Number code**: 3 = ع, 7 = ح, 9 = ق, 5 or kh = خ, gh = غ, 2 = ء. (In Algeria 9 is ق, never ص.)
2. **Letter pairs**: ch = ش, kh = خ, gh = غ, dj or j = ج, ou = و (long u), th = ث (rare, most say t).
3. **Double letters** show a strong (doubled) sound: bezzaf, khalles, ddar, ssou9.
4. **Vowels**: a, i, ou, e (short) — no fixed rule, people write as they hear.
5. **French words stay French**: livraison, commande, taille, colis, d'accord.
6. **Apostrophe** sometimes for "the": l'karhba, l'kho.

### 6.3 Many spellings, one word — accept them all

| Main form (this book) | Other ways people type it |
|---|---|
| wach | wesh, wech, wsh, wash |
| ch7al | chhal, ch'hal, sh7al, ch7el |
| bezzaf | bzaf, bezaf, bzzaf |
| dorka | dorka, drk, durka |
| 9oddam | goddam, qoddam, 9odam |
| khouya | khoya, 5ouya, kho |
| sa7a | saha, sa7a, sahha |
| ya3tik sa7a | yatik saha, y3tik sa7a, yaatik saha |
| inchallah | nchallah, inshallah, incha allah |
| l7amdoullah | hamdoulah, l7amdolah, hmd |
| ma3labalich | ma3labalich, mlablich, ma3labalich |
| mazal | mazel, mazal, mzl |
| kayen | kain, kayn, kayen |
| makach | makanch, makach, mkch |
| normal | nrml, normal |
| bessah | bessa7, bsah, bessah |
| kifach | kifeh, kifech, kifach |
| 3lach | 3lah, alach, 3lech |
| wa9tach | wa9tah, waktach, wa9tech |
| tkhalles | tkhales, t5alles |
| mli7 | mlih, mli7 |

**Rule for the AI**: understand every spelling; when writing, use one clean, consistent spelling (the main form).

### 6.4 Text style

- Short messages, few full stops, emojis are common (😂 🙏 ❤️ 👌 🔥 🇩🇿).
- Laughing: hhhh / hhhhhh (not "haha").
- Many people mix both alphabets in one message.
- Capital letters are rare; don't shout in capitals.

---

# Chapter 7 — Standard Arabic (Fus7a) → Algiers Darija

AI tools often slip into Standard Arabic (الفصحى). Algerians find it cold, like a letter from the ministry. Use this table to swap every Fus7a word for the Darija one. French is added because many Algerians think in French.

### 7.1 Question words

| Fus7a | French | Darija (Arabic) | Darija (Latin) | English |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ماذا؟ / ما؟ | quoi ? | واش؟ | wach? | what? |
| ما هذا؟ | c'est quoi ? | واش هذا؟ | wach hada? | what is this? |
| من؟ | qui ? | شكون؟ | chkoun? | who? |
| أين؟ | où ? | وين؟ | win? | where? |
| من أين؟ | d'où ? | منين؟ | mnin? | from where? |
| إلى أين؟ | où (vas-tu) ? | وين رايح؟ | win rayeh? | where are you going? |
| متى؟ | quand ? | وقتاش؟ | wa9tach? | when? |
| لماذا؟ | pourquoi ? | علاش؟ | 3lach? | why? |
| كيف؟ | comment ? | كيفاش؟ | kifach? | how? |
| كم؟ | combien ? | شحال؟ | ch7al? | how much / how many? |
| كم الثمن؟ | c'est combien ? | بشحال؟ / شحال السومة؟ | b ch7al? / ch7al essouma? | how much is it? |
| أيّ؟ | lequel ? | أناهو؟ / أناهي؟ | anahou? / anahi? | which one? |
| هل …؟ | est-ce que … ? | واش …؟ | wach …? | (yes/no question) |
| لمن هذا؟ | c'est à qui ? | تاع من هذا؟ | ta3 men hada? | whose is this? |
| ماذا تريد؟ | tu veux quoi ? | واش تحب؟ | wach t7ab? | what do you want? |
| ماذا حدث؟ | qu'est-ce qui s'est passé ? | واش صرا؟ | wach sra? | what happened? |
| ما بك؟ | qu'est-ce que tu as ? | واش بيك؟ | wach bik? | what's wrong with you? |
| كيف حالك؟ | comment ça va ? | كيراك؟ / واش راك؟ | kirak? / wach rak? | how are you? |
| ما اسمك؟ | comment tu t'appelles ? | واش سميتك؟ | wach smitek? | what's your name? |
| كم عمرك؟ | quel âge as-tu ? | شحال عمرك؟ | ch7al 3omrek? | how old are you? |
| كم الساعة؟ | quelle heure est-il ? | شحال الساعة؟ | ch7al ssa3a? | what time is it? |

| من أين أنت؟ | tu es d'où ? | منين راك؟ | mnin rak? | where are you from? |

| المنتج غير متوفّر حالياً | produit indisponible | ماكانش درك / كمل | makach dorka / kmel | not available now |
| نعتذر عن التأخير | désolés pour le retard | سمحلنا على الروطار | sme7lna 3la retard | sorry for the delay |
| هل تريد تأكيد الطلب؟ | vous confirmez la commande ? | تحب نكونفيرميو الكوموند؟ | t7ab nconfirmiw la commande? | shall we confirm the order? |
| سيتّصل بك المندوب | le livreur va vous appeler | الليفرور يعيطلك | le livreur y3ayetlek | the courier will call you |
| شكراً لثقتكم | merci pour votre confiance | يعطيكم الصحة على الثقة | ya3tikom sa7a 3la tti9a | thanks for your trust |
| نحن في خدمتكم | nous sommes à votre service | رانا هنا | rana hna | we're here for you |
| هل هناك شيء آخر؟ | autre chose ? | كاش حاجة أخرى؟ | kach 7aja okhra? | anything else? |
| تمّ إلغاء الطلب | commande annulée | تانولات الكوموند | tannulat la commande | the order was cancelled |
| استبدال المنتج | échange du produit | التبديل / ليشونج | ttabdil / l'échange | exchange |
| استرجاع المبلغ | remboursement | نرجعولك دراهمك | nraj3oulek drahmek | we give you your money back |

**Make it stronger**: bezzaf (very), ga3 (completely), ghaya (super), "mli7 bezzaf", "ghali bezzaf", "chbab ghaya", "kbir kbir" (doubling = very).

# Chapter 15 — Names: people, places and brands

### 15.1 Asking for names

| Latin | Arabic | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| wach smitek? / kifach smitek? | واش سميتك؟ / كيفاش سميتك؟ | what's your name? |
| smiti … | سميتي … | my name is … |
| smiya | السمية | name (first name or full name) |
| lism / le prénom | الاسم / البرينوم | first name |
| la9ab / le nom | اللقب / النوم | surname (family name) |
| smiya kamla | السمية الكاملة | full name |
| smitou / smitha | سميتو / سميتها | his name / her name |
| bach nsemmik? ⚠️ | — | what should I call you? |

On forms, **"Nom" = surname (la9ab)** and **"Prénom" = first name (lism)**. Algerians often say the surname first ("Benali Karim").

### 15.2 Titles in front of names

| Title | Arabic | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| Si + name | سي | respectful "Mr" for a man ("Si Mohamed") |
| 3ammi + name | عمي | older man ("3ammi Rabah") |
| Khalti + name | خالتي | older woman ("khalti Fatma") |
| el Hadj / el Hadja + name | الحاج / الحاجة | older man / woman (respect, not only pilgrims) |
| Cheikh | الشيخ | old man / religious teacher / chaabi master |
| Lalla + name ⚠️ | لالة | respectful for a woman (older use) |
| Madame / Monsieur | مدام / مسيو | Mrs / Mr |
| Docteur / Maître | دوكتور / ميتر | doctor / lawyer |
| Prof / Oustad | أستاذ | teacher |

### 15.3 Common Algerian first names (men)

Mohamed (Moh, Hamou ⚠️), Ahmed (Hmida ⚠️), Abdelkader (Kader), Abderrahmane (Abdou), Abdelkrim / Karim (Krimo), Sid Ahmed (Sidou), Sid Ali (Sidali), Mustapha, Omar, Ali, Said, Rachid, Kamel, Djamel, Farid, Nabil, Mourad, Hichem, Lotfi, Rafik, Toufik, Redouane, Nadir, Salim, Samir, Sofiane, Walid, Riad, Yacine, Amine, Mehdi, Nassim, Bilel, Islam, Hamza, Ilyes / Lyes, Anis, Adel, Fares, Oussama, Ayoub, Aymen, Zakaria (Zaki), Abdelhak, Abdellah, Youcef, Brahim, Smail, Rabah, Ammar, Messaoud, Larbi, Boualem, Yanis, Massinissa (Massi), Koceila, Idir, Juba, Mohand, Arezki, Lounes, Akli.

### 15.4 Common Algerian first names (women)

Fatima (Fatma), Khadidja (Dija, Khadoudja), Aïcha, Amina, Meriem (Mimi), Yasmine, Sarah, Lina, Ines, Imene, Rania, Houda, Nesrine, Asma, Amel, Kenza, Chahinez, Ryma, Manel, Feriel, Selma, Racha, Djihane, Hadjer, Maroua, Aya, Malak, Assil, Fella, Wafa, Zineb, Nadia, Samira, Souad, Karima, Naima, Malika, Leila, Yamina, Zohra, Ouarda, Djamila, Fadila, Ghania, Hassina, Saliha, Sonia, Lydia, Katia, Kahina, Thiziri, Tinhinane, Dihia, Ouardia.

**Careful**: some names can be for men or women (Nour, Wissem ⚠️, Nihad ⚠️). If you are not sure, use the polite plural (rakom, t7abou) or "vous".

### 15.5 Family names (patterns)

- **Ben-** / **Bel-** (son of): Benali, Benamar, Belkacem, Belhadj.
- **Bou-** (father of / the one with): Bouzid, Boumediene, Bouchama.
- **Aït-** (Kabyle, "the family of"): Aït Ahmed, Aït Ali.
- **Ould-** (son of): Ould Ali, Ould Kaddour.
- Ending **-i**: Hamidi, Mansouri, Saidi.
Never guess a person's gender from the family name.

| f lfjer / f lmaghreb / f le3cha | في الفجر / في المغرب / في العشا | at dawn / at sunset / at night prayer time |

### 21.4 Calendar

- **Months**: Algerians mostly say the French names (janvier, février, mars, avril, mai, juin, juillet, août, septembre, octobre, novembre, décembre) or the **number**: "chhar wa7ed" (January), "chhar tes3a" (September), "f chhar 12" (in December).
- Old farming month names (older people, ⚠️): yennayer, fourar, mars, ibrir, mayou, younyou, youlyouz, ghouchet, choutamber, ktouber, nouamber, doujamber.
- Islamic months used for religion: Cha3ban, Ramdan, Chawwal, Dhou l7ijja, Mo7arram…
- **this / last / next**: had l3am / l3am lli fat / l3am jay (this / last / next year) · had chhar / chhar lli fat / chhar jay · had simana / simana lli fatet / simana jaya.
- lwiken / la fin de semaine (Friday + Saturday) · 3otla / congé (day off) · kul youm (every day) · kul simana (every week) · men ba3d (later) · 9bila (a little earlier, just now) ⚠️.

# Chapter 22 — Street talk and slang (Algiers)

Use these to **understand** people and to sound natural in casual posts. In business replies, keep it clean and polite.

| Arabic | Latin | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| يا خو | ya kho | bro! |
| يا صاحبي | ya sa7bi | my friend |
| يا الشيخ | ya chikh | (friendly) mate / sir |
| ياو | yaw | come on! / hey! (Algiers) |
| يالله | yallah | let's go / hurry up |
| أرواح | arwa7 | come here |
| روح | rou7 | go away / go |
| نورمال | normal | sure, no problem, of course (used ALL the time) |
| مريقل / مريڨل | mriguel | sorted, fine, cool ("koulech mriguel" = all good) |
| غاية | ghaya | excellent, top ("ghaya!") |
| شباب | chbab | nice, beautiful (things), great |
| مليح / مليحة | mli7 / mli7a | good |
| خايب | khayeb | bad / ugly |
| هبال / مهبول | hbal / mahboul | crazy |
| ما فيهاش | ma fihach | no big deal |
| أدخل سوق راسك | odkhol sou9 rassek | mind your own business |
| واش دخلك؟ | wach dakhlek? | what's it to you? |
| خاطيني | khatini | not my problem / leave me out of it |
| الحقرة | l7ogra | contempt, unfair treatment |
| يدير الشو | ydir le show | he's showing off |
| كذاب | kadab | liar |
| خرطي | khorti | lie / nonsense ("khorti!" = that's a lie) — Algiers |
| شكون قالك؟ | chkoun 9allek? | who told you? |
| ما كاش منها | makach menha | no way / that doesn't exist |
| كاين ولا ماكانش؟ | kayen wella makach? | is it there or not? |
| واش بيك؟ | wach bik? | what's wrong with you? |
| خليني | khallini | leave me alone |
| ما تزيدش | ma tzidch | don't push it / stop |
| يزي / بزاف عليك | yezzi | enough! (also eastern) |
| رانا هنا | rana hna | we're here (for you) |
| الله غالب | allah ghaleb | it is what it is |
| ديرها في بالك | dirha fi balek | keep it in mind |
| على بالي / على بالك؟ | 3la bali / 3la balek? | I know / you know? |
| زعما زعما | za3ma za3ma | "as if…" (doubt, irony) |
| لهنا وبرك | lhna w bark | that's it, that's all |
| تبزنيس | tbezness | to deal / sell informally |
| يحوس | y7aws | to look for / to wander ("rani n7aws 3la…" = I'm looking for…) — Algiers |
| نحوس على | n7aws 3la | I'm looking for |
| تسلك | tsalek | to manage / get by ("rani nsalek" = I'm getting by) |
| دبر راسك | debber rassek | sort yourself out |
| الشيطانة | chitana | cleverness / trick |
| فايق | fay9 | smart / alert |
| زلط | zalet | broke (no money) ⚠️ |
| ميزيريا | la misère | hard life / misery |
| الحرقة / حراق | l7arga / 7arrag | illegal emigration by boat / migrant (serious topic, use carefully) |

Vulgar words and insults are left out on purpose. The AI should understand that people may swear, but it should never write insults back.

# Chapter 24 — Algerian proverbs (lamthal)

Folk sayings people use in conversation. ⚠️ Wording varies by family and region.

| Latin | Arabic | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Li fat mat | اللي فات مات | what's past is dead (let it go) |
| Kul khenfous 3and ommou ghzal | كل خنفوس عند امو غزال | every beetle is a gazelle to its mother |
| Yed wa7da ma tsaffe9ch | يد وحدة ما تصفقش | one hand can't clap (teamwork) |
| Lbab li yjik menou ri7, seddou w strayya7 | الباب اللي يجيك منو الريح سدو واستريح | the door that brings you wind, close it and rest |
| Kul ta2khira fiha khira | كل تأخيرة فيها خيرة | every delay hides something good |
| Dir lkhir w nsa | دير الخير وأنسى | do good and forget it |
| L3in ma tetla3ch 3la l7ajeb | العين ما تطلعش على الحاجب | the eye doesn't rise above the eyebrow (respect elders) |
| Li ma 3andou drahem, klamou masset | اللي ما عندو دراهم كلامو ماسط | the man with no money: his words have no taste |
| Chouf lom w khoud lbent | شوف الأم وخود البنت | look at the mother, then marry the daughter |
| Li 7far 7ofra l khouh ytih fiha | اللي حفر حفرة لخوه يطيح فيها | who digs a hole for his brother falls in it |
| Ma ydoum 7al | ما يدوم حال | nothing stays the same |
| Sber mefta7 lfaraj | الصبر مفتاح الفرج | patience is the key to relief |
| Lqerch labyadh l nhar lak7al | القرش الأبيض للنهار الأكحل | the white coin for the black day (save money) |
| Kul wa7ed w zahrou | كل واحد وزهرو | everyone has his own luck |
| Li ma ychouf mel ghorbal a3ma | اللي ما يشوف من الغربال أعمى | who can't see through a sieve is blind (it's obvious) |
| Kif jit kif ma jitch | كيف جيت كيف ما جيتش | coming or not, no difference |
| Lbegra ki tti7 ykatrou jazzarine | البقرة كي تطيح يكثرو الجزارين | when the cow falls, the butchers multiply |
| Lmektoub ma menouch hroub | المكتوب ما منوش هروب | you can't run from fate |
| Li y7ab zzin ysber l t9ib lwdnin | اللي يحب الزين يصبر لتقيب الودنين | who wants beauty must bear the ear piercing |
| Ana w khouya 3la wlad 3ammi, w ana w wlad 3ammi 3la lberrani | أنا وخويا على ولاد عمي، وأنا وولاد عمي على البراني | me and my brother against cousins, me and cousins against strangers |

# Chapter 25 — Words with a special Algerian meaning

### 25.1 Same word, different meaning (be careful!)

| Word | In Algeria | Elsewhere |
|---|---|---|
| l3afia (العافية) | **fire** | health / strength (Middle East). So never write "ya3tik l3afia" to an Algerian: say **ya3tik sa7a** |
| sa7a (صحا) | thanks / OK / well done | health |
| chira (شيرة) | girl / young woman (slang) | in Morocco: hashish ⚠️ |
| drahem (دراهم) | money (main word) | Morocco: dirhams (currency) |
| dorka (درك) | now | — (Moroccan "daba", Tunisian "taw") |
| bezzaf (بزاف) | a lot | same in Morocco; Tunisia says "barcha" |
| koulech (كلش) | everything (Algiers) | "koulchi" in Morocco and western Algeria |
| zin (زين) | beautiful (person) | Morocco "zwin" |
| mli7 (مليح) | good | Morocco "mzyan", Tunisia "behi" |
| safi (صافي) | OK / that's it (west, some Algiers) | very Moroccan; Algiers prefers "khlas", "c'est bon" |
| yekhessni (يخصني) | I need | Morocco "khassni" |
| machi (ماشي) | not | Tunisia "mouch" |
| kayen (كاين) | there is | Tunisia "famma" |
| rou7 (روح) | go | Morocco "sir" |

# Chapter 26 — Understand all of Algeria (regions)

The AI **writes** Algiers style, but customers write from every wilaya. This chapter is to **understand** them. ⚠️ Regional words vary a lot from town to town: treat this chapter as a guide, not a rule.

### 26.1 Big sound differences

| Region | What changes | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Algiers (city) | ق said as "q" | 9al (he said), 9a3ed (sitting) |
| West, countryside, South | ق said as "g" | gal, ga3ed, goulli (tell me) |
| Tlemcen (old city) | ق often said as a soft stop ("'") | 'al (he said) ⚠️ |
| Jijel | ق often said as "k" | kal (he said) ⚠️ |
| East (Constantine, Annaba) | "nti" used for a man and a woman | "nti win rak?" said to a man |
| West (Oran) | "nta3" instead of "ta3" | nta3i (mine) |

### 26.2 West (Oran, Tlemcen, Mostaganem, Sidi Bel Abbès, Mascara…)

| Latin | Arabic | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| drouk / derk | دروك | now (Algiers: dorka) |
| ki dayer? / wech rak dayer? | كي داير؟ | how are you? |
| chriki / chrika | شريكي | my buddy (very Oran) |
| khzer | خزر | look! (Algiers: chouf) |
| 3ayen | عاين | look / watch ⚠️ |
| goul / goulli / gal | ڨول / ڨولي / ڨال | say / tell me / he said |
| ga3 | ڨاع | all |
| nta3 / nta3i | نتاع / نتاعي | of / mine (Algiers: ta3 / ta3i) |
| koulchi | كلشي | everything (Algiers: koulech) |
| ghadi | غادي | going to (future) — some areas ⚠️ |
| bghit | بغيت | I want — near the Moroccan border ⚠️ |
| kifech | كيفاش | how |
| wah / ih | واه / إيه | yes |
| hadi hiya | هذي هي | that's it |
| la3chiya | العشية | afternoon / evening |
| zerda | الزردة | feast / party (also elsewhere) |
| kho / ya kho | خو | bro |

### 26.3 East (Constantine, Annaba, Sétif, Batna, Skikda, Guelma, Souk Ahras…)

| Latin | Arabic | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| chbik? | شبيك؟ | what's wrong with you? |
| nti | نتي | you (used for men too) |
| dork / dourk | دورك | now |
| zeda | زادة | also (Algiers: tani) |
| yezzi | يزي | enough! (Algiers: barka / khlas) |
| ya weddi | يا ودي | hey man / my friend ⚠️ |
| kifech | كيفاش | how |
| ch7al | شحال | how much |
| taw | تو | now — near Tunisia (El Tarf, Souk Ahras, Tébessa) ⚠️ |
| barcha | برشا | a lot — near Tunisia ⚠️ |
| chniya / chnoua | شنية / شنوة | what — near Tunisia ⚠️ |
| mli7 / behi ⚠️ | مليح / باهي | good |
| lbare7 | البارح | yesterday |
| nhar | نهار | day |

### 26.4 South (Djelfa, Laghouat, Biskra, El Oued, Ouargla, Béchar, Adrar…)

| Latin | Arabic | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| yasser | ياسر | a lot (Algiers: bezzaf) |
| gal / goul | ڨال / ڨول | he said / say |
| zin | زين | good / beautiful |
| ya wlidi | يا وليدي | my son (to a young person) |
| ga3 | ڨاع | all |
| 3la rasi | على راسي | with pleasure |

In the South, deliveries take longer and some places have their own languages (see 51.6).

### 26.5 Kabyle (Taqbaylit) — Tizi Ouzou, Béjaïa, Bouira and many people in Algiers

Many Kabyle speakers also speak French and Darija. If someone writes in Kabyle, answer with a short Kabyle greeting and continue in French or simple Darija.

| Kabyle (Latin) | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Azul / Azul fellawen | hello / hello to you all |
| Tanemmirt / Tanemmirt aṭas | thank you / thank you very much |
| Ih / Ala | yes / no |
| Amek tellid? | how are you? |
| Labas / Bien | fine |
| Acu? | what? |
| Anda? | where? |
| Melmi? | when? |
| Acimi? | why? |
| Acḥal? | how much? |
| Yella / Ulac | there is / there isn't (nothing) |
| Ttxil-k / Ttxil-m | please (to a man / to a woman) |
| Smaḥ-iyi | sorry |
| Aṭas | a lot |
| Yelha | it's good |
| Aqcic / Taqcict | boy / girl |
| Yemma / Vava | my mother / my father |
| Axxam | house |
| Aman | water |
| Aɣrum | bread |
| Idrimen | money |
| Tamurt | the country / home village |
| Ar tufat | see you / goodbye |
| Aseggas ameggaz | happy new year (Yennayer) |

### 26.6 Other Amazigh (Berber) languages

| Language | Where | What to know |
|---|---|---|
| Chaoui (Tachawit) | Aurès: Batna, Khenchela, Oum El Bouaghi | close to Kabyle; "azul" works |
| Mozabite (Tumzabt) | Ghardaïa (M'zab valley) | people also speak Darija and French |
| Tamasheq (Tuareg) | Tamanrasset, Illizi, Djanet | people also speak Arabic / French |
| Chenoui | Tipaza, Cherchell area | Amazigh language of the coast west of Algiers |

### 26.7 How the AI should use this

1. **Understand** regional words, but **answer** in Algiers style.
2. Never correct a customer's dialect, never make jokes about a region.
3. "Bghit", "daba", "barcha", "taw" in a message usually mean the customer is from the far west or the far east (or from Morocco / Tunisia). Answer normally in Algiers Darija.
4. If a Kabyle speaker writes "Azul", answer "Azul! Tanemmirt 🙏" and continue in French or simple Darija.

# Chapter 27 — French inside Darija (how Algerians mix)

Algerians in Algiers mix French in almost every sentence. This is **normal** and makes text sound real. Rules:

1. French nouns get the Darija article "el / l": **la commande** → "l commande", "el colis", "la livraison".
2. French verbs get Darija endings:
   - commander → **ncommandi** (I order), **tcommandi** (you order), **commandit** (I ordered)
   - confirmer → **nconfirmi**, **confirmit**
   - annuler → **nannuli**, **annulit**
   - envoyer → **nenvoyi**
   - payer → (people use **nkhalles** more)
   - charger → **ncharji** (charge the phone)
   - bloquer → **nbloqui**
   - partager → **npartagi**
   - télécharger → **ntelechargi**
   - réserver → **nreservi**
   - stresser → **mstressi** (stressed)
   - garer → **nparki** (park)
3. Very common French words in Darija: normal, d'accord, c'est bon, déjà, surtout, bien sûr, franchement, la vérité, en tout cas, donc, mais, voilà, bon, ça y est, pas de problème, ça va, au moins, presque, vraiment, exprès.
   - "Franchement, chbab!" = Honestly, it's great!
   - "Déjà chrit wa7ed" = I already bought one.
   - "En tout cas ya3tik sa7a" = Anyway, thanks.
4. Text shortcuts (French SMS style, very used in Algeria):

| Short | Means |
|---|---|
| slt | salut (hi) |
| cv | ça va |
| stp / svp | please |
| mrc / mer6 | merci |
| dcr / dac | d'accord (OK) |
| b1 | bien (good) |
| r1 | rien (nothing) |
| 2m1 | demain (tomorrow) |
| dsl | désolé (sorry) |
| psk / pcq | parce que (because) |
| mnt | maintenant (now) |
| tjr | toujours (always) |
| bcp | beaucoup (a lot) |
| pk | pourquoi (why) |
| jsp | je ne sais pas (I don't know) |
| mdr / hhh / hhhh | lol (Algerians write "hhhhh") |
| tbh / jk | (rare) |
| f privé / pv / mp | message privé (DM). Algerians say **"ab3atli f privé"**, not "inbox" |
| up | push the post up |
| dispo | disponible (available) |
| px / prx | prix (price) |

5. **Mirror rule for the AI**: if the person writes in French, answer in French (maybe with a little Darija). If they write Darija in Latin letters, answer the same. If they write in Arabic letters, answer in Arabic letters. Never force Standard Arabic.

# Chapter 28 — TV, Ramadan and humour words

### 28.1 The shows people quote
Algerian TV comedy, mostly shown during Ramadan after iftar, shapes how people joke online. Well-known examples (good to recognise when people quote them):
- **Nass Mlah City** (ناس ملاح سيتي) — classic 2000s sitcom by Djaffar Gacem, still quoted.
- **Djemai Family** (جمعي فاميلي) — family sitcom, Algiers humour.
- **Achour El Acher** (عاشور العاشر) — royal comedy series by Djaffar Gacem, very popular in Ramadan.
- **Imarat El Hadj Lakhdar** (عمارة الحاج لخضر) — building/neighbours comedy.
- **Dakyous & Makyous** (دقيوس ومقيوس) — Ramadan comedy.
- **El Khawa** (الخاوة) — drama series ("the brothers").

The AI should **not** invent quotes from these shows. If someone quotes a line, react warmly ("hhhh normal!") without pretending to know the episode.

### 28.2 How Algerian humour sounds
- Lots of **"hhhhhh"** (laughing). 3 to 10 h's.
- Self-mocking about prices, traffic, power cuts, administration: "lbled hadi…" (this country…), "kima dima" (as always).
- Exaggeration: "mout b dé7k" (dying of laughter), "rani mout" (I'm dead / so tired), "tla3li dem" (my blood went up = I got angry).
- Teasing with "ya kho", "ya ch-chikh", "wach rak t9oul?" (what are you saying?!).
- Short reactions: **"Ya rabbi!"** (oh God!), **"Ya latif!"** (oh my!), **"Hadi hiya!"** (that's it exactly!), **"Ma ndirch fik thi9a"** (I don't trust you — joking), **"Normal kho"**, **"Top"**, **"Ghaya"**, **"Bravo 3lik"**.
- Emojis Algerians use a lot: 😂 🤣 🔥 ❤️ 👌 🙏 🇩🇿 💯 😍 🤲.

# Chapter 30 — Shopping and delivery words

### 30.1 Prices: Algerians talk in CENTIMES ⚠️ (very important)

1 dinar = 100 centimes. People often say the price in centimes. **Divide by 100** to get dinars.

| What people say | Arabic | Real price |
|---|---|---|
| alf | ألف | 10 DA |
| 3achra alaf | عشرة آلاف | 100 DA |
| khamsin alf | خمسين ألف | 500 DA |
| mya alf | مية ألف | 1 000 DA |
| mitin alf | ميتين ألف | 2 000 DA |
| mitin w khamsin (alf) | ميتين وخمسين | 2 500 DA |
| tlatmya w tes3in (alf) | ثلاث مية وتسعين | 3 900 DA |
| nos mlyoun | نص مليون | 5 000 DA |
| mlyoun | مليون | 10 000 DA |
| mlyoun w mitin | مليون وميتين | 12 000 DA |
| mlyoun w nos | مليون ونص | 15 000 DA |
| zouj mlayen | زوج ملاين | 20 000 DA |
| 3achra mlayen | عشرة ملاين | 100 000 DA |

How to read it:
- "390" typed for an item that costs 3 900 DA usually means "390 alf" = 3 900 DA, not a joke offer.
- "khamsa alaf" can mean 50 DA (centimes) or 5 000 DA (dinars). "Tqsed 5000 DA?" = You mean 5 000 DA?
- If someone says "alf dinar", that's 1 000 DA (they said dinar).
- Written prices use digits + DA or دج: "3 900 DA", "3900 دج".

# Chapter 31 — Cash on delivery words (الدفع عند الاستلام / paiement à la livraison)

Vocabulary only. Every word is in Arabic letters, Latin letters (Darija + French), and meaning.

| الحبات / المونية | les pièces / la monnaie | coins |
| نخلص كامل | nkhalles kamel | I pay all of it |
| نخلص النص | nkhalles nnoss | I pay half |
| نزيدك / ننقصك | nzidek / nna9sek | I add / I lower (for you) |
| غلطة في الحساب | ghalta f l7sab | mistake in the total |
| الفاكتورة / البون / الريسو | la facture / le bon / le reçu | invoice / slip / receipt |
| البوردورو | le bordereau | courier shipping slip (with the amount on it) |
| المبلغ مكتوب على الكولي | lmablagh mektoub 3la le colis | the amount is written on the parcel |

Prices are often **said in centimes** (see Chapter 30): "mitin alf" = 2 000 DA, "nos mlyoun" = 5 000 DA.

### 31.3 Verbs (pay, receive, deliver) with forms

| Meaning | I | you (m) | you (f) | he / she | we | you (pl) | they |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| pay | nkhalles | tkhalles | tkhallsi | ykhalles / tkhalles | nkhallsou | tkhallsou | ykhallsou |
| receive | nestalem | testalem | testalmi | yestalem / testalem | nestalmou | testalmou | yestalmou |
| deliver / bring to | nwassal | twassal | twassli | ywassal / twassal | nwasslou | twasslou | ywasslou |
| arrive (to you) | — | tousslek (it reaches you) | tousslek | yousslek / tousslek | — | tousselkom | — |
| hand over | nsalem | tsalem | tsalmi | ysalem | nsalmou | tsalmou | ysalmou |
| refuse | nrfodh / nrefusi | trefusi | trefusi | yrefusi | nrefusiw | trefusiw | yrefusiw |
| open (parcel) | nfte7 | tfte7 | tfet7i | yfte7 | nfet7ou | tfet7ou | yfet7ou |
| check | nverifi / nchouf | tverifi | tverifi | yverifi | nverifiw | tverifiw | yverifiw |
| wait | nestenna | testenna | testennay | yestenna | nestennaw | testennaw | yestennaw |

Past: **khallast** (I paid), **khallas** (he paid), **khallset** (she paid) · **stalamt / tsalamt** (I received) · **wsslatni** (it reached me) · **rfadt / refusit** (I refused) · **jabha** (he brought it).

Arabic: خلصت، خلص، خلصت · ستلمت / تسلمت ⚠️ · وصلتني · رفضت / ريفوزيت · جابها.

# Chapter 33 — Everyday sentences

| Situation | Latin | Arabic | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| Asking someone's news | Wach rak, labas? Wach rahi la famille? | واش راك، لاباس؟ واش راهي الفاميلة؟ | How are you, all good? How's the family? |
| Saying you're busy | Rani mcharji chwiya, n3ayetlek men ba3d | راني مشارجي شوية، نعيطلك من بعد | I'm a bit busy, I'll call you later |
| Making plans | Ghodwa netla9aw f la place? | غدوة نتلاقاو في لابلاص؟ | Shall we meet tomorrow at the square? |
| Running late | Rani f ttri9, 5 min w nkoun hna | راني في الطريق، 5 دقايق ونكون هنا | I'm on the way, 5 min |
| Traffic | Bouchon kbir, ma n9derch nji f lwa9t | بوشون كبير، ما نقدرش نجي في الوقت | Big traffic jam, I can't come on time |
| Power cut | T9at3 ddaw 3andna | تقطع الضو عندنا | We have a power cut |
| Agreeing | Wah, hadi hiya! | واه، هذي هي! | Yes, exactly! |
| Disagreeing politely | Sme7li, bessah ana rayi machi hakka | سمحلي، بصح أنا رايي ماشي هكا | Sorry, but my opinion is different ⚠️ |
| Comforting | Ma t9ale9ch, koulech yetsagem in chaa Allah | ما تقلقش، كلش يتسڨم إن شاء الله | Don't worry, everything will be fixed |
| Congratulating | Mabrouk 3lik, allah ykammel b khir | مبروك عليك، الله يكمل بالخير | Congrats, may it end well |
| New baby | Mabrouk ma jak, allah ykhallih | مبروك ما جاك، الله يخليه | Congrats on the baby, God keep him |
| Someone passed away | Allah yer7mou, lbaraka fikom | الله يرحمو، البركة فيكم | God rest him, my condolences |
| Someone is sick | La bas 3lik, allah ychfik | لا باس عليك، الله يشفيك | Get well soon |
| Saying goodbye | Bslama, netla9aw | بسلامة، نتلاقاو | Bye, see you |
| Goodnight | Tesba7 3la khir | تصبح على خير | Good night |
| Leaving warmly | Rabbi m3ak / Fi aman allah | ربي معاك / في أمان الله | God be with you |
| Asking for a favour | Yer7am babak, t9der t3awenni? | يرحم باباك، تقدر تعاوني؟ | Please, can you help me? |
| Being thankful | Y3aychek khouya, ma ninsahalekch | يعيشك خويا، ما ننساهالكش | Thanks bro, I won't forget it |
| Joking | Hhhhh ya kho rak mahboul | هههه يا خو راك مهبول | Haha bro you're crazy |
| Surprise | Ya rabbi! Bessa7 ? | يا ربي! بالصح؟ | Oh God! Really? |
| Really? | Bessa7? / S7i7? | بالصح؟ / صحيح؟ | Really? / True? |
| I don't understand | Ma fhemtch, 3awedli | ما فهمتش، عاودلي | I didn't get it, repeat |
| Speak slowly | Hder bechwiya | أهدر بشوية | Talk slowly |

# Chapter 34 — Language rules for the AI

1. **Write Algiers Darija**: rani / rak, wach, win, wa9tach, ch7al, ta3, bezzaf, dorka, ghodwa, n7ab, mli7, normal, sa7a, ya3tik sa7a.
2. **Never** use Moroccan (bghit, daba, wakha, dyal, mzyan, 3afak, ghadi), Tunisian (barcha, behi, taw, 9addech, mta3, houni, zeda, nejjem) or Middle-East words (ktir, aywa, keda, ya 3omri).
3. **Mirror** the person: same letters (Arabic or Latin), same amount of French. Never switch to Standard Arabic (Fus7a) — it sounds cold and robotic.
4. **Gender**: nta / rak / t7ab for a man, nti / raki / t7abi for a woman, plural (rakom / t7abou) or French "vous" if unsure.
5. **Prices**: understand centimes (divide by 100); write prices in digits + DA / دج.
6. **Respect words**: khouya, khti, madame, 3ammi, khalti, el 7ajj. Never habibti / 3omri / zina / ya ghzala with strangers.
7. **Religious phrases** are normal and kind (inchallah, l7amdoullah, machallah, allah ybarek, allah yer7mou).
8. **"Privé", not "inbox"**: ab3atli f privé / بعثلي في البريفي.
9. **"Kamel les wilayas"**: never write 48 or 58 wilayas (Algeria now has 69).
10. **No insults**, even if the other person writes them.
11. **Grammar and spelling**: follow Chapter 5 (full grammar) and Chapter 6 (spelling). Swap any Standard Arabic word with Chapter 7. Look words up in Chapter 35 (Darija → English), 36 (English → Darija) and 37 (French → Darija).
12. **Regions**: understand the words in Chapter 26, but always answer in Algiers style.
13. **Check with a native speaker** any line marked ⚠️.

# Chapter 35 — Full dictionary A–Z (Darija → English)

Every word and short phrase in this book, in one list, sorted by the Latin spelling (French "le / la / les" is skipped when sorting). Words starting with an Arabizi number (3, 7, 9…) come first. [West], [East], [South], [Kabyle] = regional words to understand, not to write. ⚠️ = check with someone from Algiers.

**1888 entries.**
